{{DISPLAYTITLE:Glycan Structure Dictionary}}

FOR THE LATEST BETA VERSION OF GLYCAN STRUCTURE DICTIONARY SEE [https://github.com/glygener/glycan-structure-dictionary REPO] AND [https://github.com/glygener/glycan-structure-dictionary/blob/main/data/processed/dictionary_20251017_160826.json JSON FILE]  

Glycan Structure Dictionary (GSD) contains terms commonly used to describe glycan structural features in publications. The glycan structure terms are primarily sourced via automatic and manual literature mining as well as from other bioinformatics databases. The Glycan Structure Dictionary is a work in progress and the terms are updated regularly to include the new term(s) and annotations. On this Wiki page the Glycan Structure Dictionary terms are listed by term name (alphabetically) and also by glycan dictionary accessions. To submit new terms go to section [[Glycan structure dictionary#Submit new terms|Glycan Structure Dictionary#Submit new terms]]. Contact https://www.glygen.org/contact-us/ to report any issues. 

Please cite use of Glycan Structure Dictionary with: Vora J, Navelkar R, Vijay-Shanker K, Edwards N, Martinez K, Ding X, Wang T, Su P, Ross K, Lisacek F, Hayes C, Kahsay R, Ranzinger R, Tiemeyer M, Mazumder R. The glycan structure dictionary-a dictionary describing commonly used glycan structure terms. Glycobiology. 2023 Feb 17:cwad014. doi: 10.1093/glycob/cwad014. PMID: 36799723.  

==Terms (by Term Name)==

=== ABO blood group antigens ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : ABO blood group antigens <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ABO glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | ABO(H) blood group glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - ABO blood group antigens are terminal glycan structures found on glycoproteins and glycolipids that define the A, B, and H blood group types. These glycans serve as receptors for certain viruses and are specifically recognized by lectins and antibodies, playing a key role in blood group determination and immune recognition. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=Free%20glycans%20%28e.g.%2C%20milk,that%20use%20ABO%20glycans%20as%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20other%20sugar%20units%20linked%20to,diversity%20of%20recognition (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== blood group A ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group A <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000029 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00066MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00066MO G00066MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00066MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : A antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | A blood group determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | blood group A glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | blood group A trisaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino trisaccharide consisting of β-D-galactose having an α-L-fucosyl residue at the 2-position and an N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl residue at the 3-position.[CHEBI:61012] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The blood group A determinant is a glycan structure found on the surface of red blood cells that defines blood group A. It is formed by the terminal addition of N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) to the H antigen, a reaction catalyzed by A-transferase. This structure is a key component in distinguishing blood group A from other blood groups. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GalNAc(a1-3)[Fuc(a1-2)]Gal(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=O%20alleles%20at%20the%20ABO%20locus%20encode,of%20the%20A%20or%20B%20blood%20group%20antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - A readily detectable carbohydrate in the urine of two siblings with neuronal ceroid lipofuscinosis was found to be the blood group A trisaccharide.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/7249368 7249368]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Publications''' : 17 references <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10359132 10359132] <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11886841 11886841] <br>

 - ...and 7 more publications <br>
 '''Database Cross-References''' :

=== blood group A type 1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group A type 1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000030 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G66163TI <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G66163TI G66163TI] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G66163TI View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : blood group A antigen type 1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Blood group A(1) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An α-D-GalpNAc-(1→3)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→2)]-β-D-Galp-(1→3)-D-GlcpNAc having β-configuration at the reducing end anomeric centre.[CHEBI:62485] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GalNAc(a1-3)[Fuc(a1-2)]Gal(b1-3)GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - he basis of blood group A(1) and A(2) phenotypes has been debated for many decades, and still the chemical basis is unresolved. [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19121199 19121199]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== blood group A type 2 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group A type 2 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000014 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G74353FF <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G74353FF G74353FF] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G74353FF View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : A Type 2 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Blood group A type 2 chain (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprising N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine at the reducing end with a N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl-(1→3)-[α-L-fucosyl-(1→2)]-β-D-galactosyl moiety attached at the 4-position.[CHEBI:62486] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GalNAc(a1-3)[Fuc(a1-2)]Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - One hybridoma antibody (AH16), obtained on immunization with human gastric cancer cell MKN45, was directed to the blood group A determinant carried by both type 1 and type 2 chains. [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/6583282 6583282]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== blood group antigens ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group antigens <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A class of glycan epitopes found on glycoproteins and glycolipids that determine human blood groups, including the A, B, O(H), and other antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=coli.%20LPS%20consists%20of%20a%20base%20lipid,production%20in%20bacteria (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== blood group B ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group B <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : B antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | B blood group determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | blood group B glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The blood group B determinant is a specific glycan epitope, typically Galα1-3(Fucα1-2)Gal-R, present on the surface of red blood cells, glycoproteins, and glycolipids. It defines the blood group B antigen in humans and is formed by the terminal addition of galactose to the H antigen, catalyzed by B-transferase. This structure is recognized by specific antibodies and lectins, and is a key determinant in blood group typing. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=GMI-1070%2C%20targeting%20E-%2C%20P-%2C%20and,into%20%E2%80%9Cuniversal%20donor%E2%80%9D%20O%20red%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Galectin-1%20directly%20binds%20to,immune%20responses%20against%20pathogens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Similarly%2C%20blood%20group%20B,death%20from%20circulatory%20collapse (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch36/#:~:text=Galectins%20can%20also%20bind%20to,in%20the%20cytoplasm%20before%20secretion (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== blood group B type 1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group B type 1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000033 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G31734OS <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G31734OS G31734OS] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G31734OS View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : blood group B type 1 tetrasaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - α-D-Galp-(1→3)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→2)]-β-D-Galp-(1→3)-β-D-GlcpNAc with β configuration at the anomeric carbon of the GlcNAc residue at the reducing end.[CHEBI:63242] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - The minimal binding epitope was identified as the blood group H type 1 determinant (Fucα2Galβ3GlcNAc), while an optimal binding epitope was created by addition of the terminal α3-linked galactose or N-acetylgalactosamine of the blood group B type 1 determinant (Galα3(Fucα2)Galβ3GlcNAc) and the blood group A type 1 determinant (GalNAcα3(Fucα2)-Galβ3GlcNAc). [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/19208633 19208633]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== blood group H ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group H <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00068MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00068MO G00068MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00068MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : blood group H glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | H antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | H blood group determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The blood group H determinant is a glycan antigen and epitope characterized by the presence of an α1-2-linked fucose residue, synthesized by α1-2 fucosyltransferase (FucT). It is formed by the addition of α1-2-linked fucose to the terminal galactose of a type 1 unit (Galβ1-3GlcNAc), resulting in the structure Fucα1-2Galβ1-3GlcNAc. This determinant serves as a precursor for ABO blood group antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Fuc(a1-2)Gal(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=For%20the%20UDP-Gal%20transporter%20from,Lex%20and%20SLex%20%28for%20the%20%CE%B11-3%20FucT%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=The%20Lewis%20b%20antigen%20%28Leb%29%20is,same%20epithelia%20that%20express%20FUT2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== blood group H type 2 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : blood group H type 2 <br>

=== P blood group antigens ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : P blood group antigens <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : P-blood group (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - P blood group antigens are glycan structures, specifically glycosphingolipids, present on red blood cells. They serve as targets for specific antibodies, influencing blood group compatibility, and also act as receptors for certain bacterial adhesins, playing a role in susceptibility to urinary tract infections. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== core 1 O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core 1 O-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000186 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00031MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00031MO G00031MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00031MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : C1G (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | core 1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | core 1 O-GalNAc glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Galβ1-3GalNAcαS/T (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The core 1 O-glycan, also known as Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-O-Ser/Thr, is a disaccharide structure that forms the basic core of mucin-type O-glycans in vertebrates and some invertebrates. It consists of a galactose β1-3 linked to N-acetylgalactosamine α-linked to serine or threonine residues on proteins. This structure is the most common O-GalNAc glycan, generated by the enzyme C1GALT1 (T-synthase), and serves as a precursor for further elongation and modification, including sialylation and sulfation, in O-glycosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== core 2 O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core 2 O-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000046 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - The trisaccharide β-D-Gal-(1→3)-[β-D-GlcNAc-(1→6)]-D-GalNAc with α configuration at the anomeric centre of the N-acetylglucosamine residue at the reducing end.[CHEBI:62158] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Core 2 O-glycan is a mucin-type O-GalNAc glycan structure formed by the addition of a GlcNAcβ1-6 branch to the GalNAc residue of core 1, resulting in the structure Galβ1-3(GlcNAcβ1-6)GalNAcα1-O-Ser/Thr. This structure is a major branch of O-GalNAc glycans, is more cell type–specific than core 1, and serves as a scaffold for further extension and modification, including the formation of selectin ligands. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :

=== core 4 O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core 4 O-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000048 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00037MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00037MO G00037MO] <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino trisaccharide comprising an N -acetyl-α-D-galactosamine residue at the reducing end, to which are (1→3)- and (1→6)-linked two N -acetyl-α-D-glucosamine residues.[CHEBI:71341] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Core 4 O-GalNAc glycan is a mucin-type O-glycan structure where the core 3 structure (GlcNAcβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) is further branched by the addition of a GlcNAc residue via a β1-6 linkage, resulting in the structure GlcNAcβ1-6(GlcNAcβ1-3)GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GlcNAc(b1-3)[GlcNAc(b1-6)]GalNAc(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=There%20are%20two%20different%20types%20of,the%20text.%20Green%20lines%20are%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== core fucose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core fucose <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000049 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G78059CC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G78059CC G78059CC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G78059CC View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : 6-linked Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | a1-6 Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | a6-linked Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Core Fuc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Fuc a1-6 to chitobiose core (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Fuc a1-6 to internal GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Fuca6 to chitobiose core (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Fuca6 to internal GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Modification of the N-glycan core structure with fucose moiety attached at the innermost GlcNAc by FUT8 enzyme. Core Fuc is found on paucimannose, hybrid and complex type N-glycans. [https://doi.org/10.1016/bs.acr.2014.11.006]. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A fucose residue attached in α1-6 linkage to the innermost GlcNAc of N-glycans, serving as a key structural feature that influences glycan function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Required for signaling through TCR (Source:  29434598) <br>
 - VGF, PDGFr, and EGFr and TGFbeta receptor dysregulation in Fut8 KO mice (Source: 17132494) <br>
 - IgG-Fcg receptor interactions destabilized by core Fuc (Source: 29062024) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Emphysema (Source: unknown) <br>
 - systemic lupus erythematosuslung cancer (Source: unknown) <br>
 - autoimmunity (Source: unknown) <br>
 - tumor metastasis (Source: unknown) <br>

 - The approach was validated for selective capture and analysis of core fucosylated N-glycans present in complex glycan mixtures derived from mammalian serum IgG.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27678371 27678371]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== core M1 O-mannose glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core M1 O-mannose glycan <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G85856KC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G85856KC G85856KC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G85856KC View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : core M1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | M1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Core M1 O-mannose glycan is one of the three main core structures (M1–M3) of O-mannose glycans, characterized by the addition of a single N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) in β1-2 linkage to a protein-linked mannose. This core structure is generated by the action of POMGNT1 and can be further extended by various glycosyltransferases, representing a specific and conserved oligosaccharide core in O-mannosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GlcNAc(b1-2)Man(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Biosynthetic%20pathway%20of%20O-mannose,and%20sialyltransferases (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Core%20M1%20glycans%20can%20be%20further,that%20adds%20%CE%B21-3GalNAc%20to%20the%20GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=O-Man%20glycan%20biosynthetic%20pathway.,DPM2%2C%20and%20DPM3%20for%20Dol-P-Man%5D%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== core M2 O-mannose glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core M2 O-mannose glycan <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G56868BH <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G56868BH G56868BH] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G56868BH View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : core M2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | M2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Core M2 O-mannose glycan is a type of O-mannose glycan core structure characterized by the addition of two N-acetylglucosamines (GlcNAc) to a protein-linked mannose via β1-2 and β1-6 linkages. This structure is formed from core M1 through the action of MGAT5B and can be further extended by various glycosyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GlcNAc(b1-2)[GlcNAc(b1-6)]Man(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Biosynthetic%20pathway%20of%20O-mannose,and%20sialyltransferases (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Core%20M1%20glycans%20can%20be%20further,that%20adds%20%CE%B21-3GalNAc%20to%20the%20GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== core M3 O-mannose glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : core M3 O-mannose glycan <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G97041TX <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G97041TX G97041TX] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G97041TX View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : core M3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | M3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The core M3 O-mannose glycan is one of the three main core structures (M1–M3) of O-mannose glycans, representing a specific conserved oligosaccharide core found on mammalian α-dystroglycan. It serves as a priming substrate for the addition of repeating disaccharide units (Xyl-α3-GlcA-β3-)n, a process catalyzed by the LARGE1 or LARGE2 glycosyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GlcNAc(b1-4)Man(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=This%20action%20serves%20to%20prime%20the,have%20disaccharide-modified%20Hyl (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=O-Man%20glycan%20biosynthetic%20pathway.,DPM2%2C%20and%20DPM3%20for%20Dol-P-Man%5D%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=This%20disaccharide%20is%20a%20substrate,%28or%20LARGE2%29%20glycosyltransferase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch13/#:~:text=Core%20M1%20glycans%20can%20be%20further,that%20adds%20%CE%B21-3GalNAc%20to%20the%20GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''term (main_entry)''' : N-acetyllactosamine (type 1) <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000181 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00056MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00056MO G00056MO] <br>

 '''Evidence''' :
 - The N-acetyllactosamine type 1 (LacNAc type 1, Galβ1,3GlcNAc) is a well-known precursor of several important blood group epitopes, such as Lewis A, Lewis B, or sialyl Lewis A [1], which are involved in many biological processes, e.g., fertilization [2] and pathogen adhesion [3]. [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28796164 28796164]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== N-acetyllactosamine (type 2) ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : N-acetyllactosamine (type 2) <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000129 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00055MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00055MO G00055MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00055MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : LacNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Type 2 LN (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - N-acetyllactosamine is a beta-D-galactopyranosyl-(1→4)-N-acetyl-D-glucosamine having beta-configuration at the reducing end anomeric centre. It is also known as galb1-4glcnacb or lacnac, belongs to the class of organic compounds known as acylaminosugars. These are organic compounds containing a sugar linked to a chain through N-acyl group. N-Acetyllactosamine is an extremely weak basic (essentially neutral) compound (based on its pKa). N-Acetyllactosamine exists in all living organisms, ranging from bacteria to humans.[CHEBI:16153,HMDB0001542] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - Utilising a fast and sensitive screening method based on imidazolium-tagged probes, we report unprecedented reversible activity of bacterial beta1,4-galactosyltransferases to catalyse the transgalactosylation from lactose to N-acetylglucosamine to form N-acetyllactosamine in the presence of UDP.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31165848 31165848]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== GA1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GA1 <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GA1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : asialo-GM1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Ganglio-series asialylated (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Asialo-GM1, also known as GA1, is a neutral ganglioside of the ganglio-series with the structure Galβ1-3GalNAcβ1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer. It is formed by extending lactosylceramide with GalNAc (β1-4) followed by Gal (β1-3). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== GD1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GD1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000082 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G81983KG <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G81983KG G81983KG] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G81983KG View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GD1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | GD1c (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GD1 refers to a class of acidic glycosphingolipids known as gangliosides that contain two sialic acid residues. These molecules are commonly found in neural tissues, and the GD1 group includes specific members such as GD1a and GD1b. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20a%20critical%20event%20in%20tumor,are%20directed%20by%20O-GlcNAcylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Hormonal and metabolic disturbances observed in both adult and paediatric patients with Gaucher disease type 1 (GD1) are discussed in this review. The PubMed database was used to identify articles on endocrine and metabolic disorders in GD1.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/31791361 31791361]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== GM1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GM1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000091 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G48558GR <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G48558GR G48558GR] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G48558GR View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GM1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | GM1 ganglioside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | GM1-ganglioside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : GM1a (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino pentasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence β-D-Gal-(1→3)-β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to the inner galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. The oligosaccharide of ganglioside GM1a.[CHEBI:59208] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GM1 is a ganglioside, a type of glycosphingolipid containing one sialic acid residue. It consists of a ceramide lipid linked to a pentasaccharide glycan headgroup, which is recognized by proteins such as cholera toxin. GM1 is formed by the addition of galactose to GM2 and plays important roles as a receptor for cholera toxin and in cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)[Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The branched pentasaccharide chain of ganglioside GM1 is a prominent cell surface ligand, for example, for cholera toxin or tumor growth-regulatory homodimeric galectins. (Source: 16267866) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - GM1 gangliosidosis (GM1) (Source:  33859490) <br>

=== GM2 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GM2 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000092 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G79389NT <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G79389NT G79389NT] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G79389NT View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GM2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino tetrasaccharide consisting of the linear sequence β-D-GalNAc-(1→4)-β-D-Gal-(1→4)-β-D-Glc having a Neu5Ac residue attached to the galactose via an α-(2→3) linkage. Corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GM2.[CHEBI:59220] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GM2 is a ganglioside, a type of glycosphingolipid containing one sialic acid residue, and is found in the brain. It serves as a biosynthetic precursor to GM1 and is characterized by a sialylated glycan structure. GM2 accumulates in certain lysosomal storage diseases such as Sandhoff disease, and its degradation requires the GM2-activator protein. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== GM3 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GM3 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000093 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G91237TK <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G91237TK G91237TK] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G91237TK View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GM3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Ganglioside GM3 (d18:1/16:0) is a glycosphingolipid (ceramide and oligosaccharide)or oligoglycosylceramide with one or more sialic acids (i.e. n-acetylneuraminic acid) linked on the sugar chain. It is a component the cell plasma membrane which modulates cell signal transduction events. Gangliosides have been found to be highly important in immunology. Ganglioside GM3 carries a net-negative charge at pH 7.0 and is acidic. Gangliosides can amount to 6% of the weight of lipids from brain, but they are found at low levels in all animal tissuesGangliosides are glycosphingolipids. There are four types of glycosphingolipids, the cerebrosides, sulfatides, globosides and gangliosides. Gangliosides are very similar to globosides except that they also contain N-acetyl neuraminic acid (NANA) in varying amounts. The specific names for the gangliosides provide information about their structure. The letter G refers to ganglioside, and the subscripts M, D, T and Q indicate that the molecule contains mono-, di-, tri and quatra-sialic acid. The numbered subscripts 1, 2 and 3 refer to the carbohydrate sequence that is attached to the ceramide. In particular, 1 stands for GalGalNAcGalGlc-ceramide, 2 stands for GalNAcGalGlc-ceramide and 3 stands for GalGlc-ceramide. Deficiencies in lysosomal enzymes that degrade the carbohydrate portions of various gangliosides are responsible for a number of lysosomal storage diseases such as Tay-Sachs disease, Sandhoff disease, and GM1 gangliosidosis. The carbohydrate portion of the ganglioside GM1 is the site of attachment of cholera toxin, the protein secreted by Vibrio cholerae.[HMDB0004844] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GM3, also known as Ganglioside GM3, is the simplest member of the ganglioside series. It consists of a ceramide linked to a sialylated lactose, containing a single sialic acid residue. GM3 serves as a precursor for more complex gangliosides and is involved in modulating insulin receptor responsiveness. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Membrane stabilizer (Source: unknown) <br>
 - Energy source (Source: unknown) <br>
 - Energy storage (Source: unknown) <br>
 - Molecular messenger: (Source: unknown) <br>
 - Signaling molecule (Source: HMDB0004844) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Gastroenteritis (Source: 35) <br>
 - Chronic gastritis (Source: 35) <br>
 - Toxoplasmosis (Source: 35) <br>
 - Peptic ulcerSugarBind_Ligand:35] (Source: unknown) <br>
 - Gastric cancer (Source: 35) <br>
 - Influenza (Source: 35) <br>
 - Diarrhea (Source: 35) <br>
 - Botulism (Source: 35) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20in%20the%20widely%20used,as%20examples%20are%20major%20brain%20GSLs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=These%20mice%20shunt%20glycosphingolipid,genetically%20outbred%20populations (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Ganglioside%20GM3%2C%20for%20example%2C,with%20embryonic%20lethality%20in%20mice (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - We also demonstrated an essential role of GM3 in murine and human auditory systems; a common pathological feature of GM3S deficiency is deafness. [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29747813 29747813]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== GT1b ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GT1b <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000101 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G40183QN <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G40183QN G40183QN] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G40183QN View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglioside GT1b (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino heptasaccharide comprising a β-D-galactosyl-(1→4)-β-D-glucose disaccharide, the galactosyl residue of which carries an N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl-(2→3)-β-D-galactosyl-(1→3)-N-acetyl-β-D-galactosaminyl trisaccharide unit and an N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl-(2→8)-N-acetyl-α-neuraminyl disaccharide unit through (2→4) and (2→3) linkages respectively. It corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GT1b.[CHEBI:59225] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GT1b is a ganglioside, which is a type of glycosphingolipid containing sialic acid. It functions as a docking site for specific proteins and toxins on neuronal cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-8)Neu5Ac(a2-3)[Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)]Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>

 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000089 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00059MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : CD77 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | ceramide trihexoside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | CTH (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Gb(3) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Gb3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Gb_3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | GL3 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | globotriaosylceramide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | P^K antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Pk antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Trihexosylceramide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Globotriaosylceramide is a type of globoside(glycosphingolipid) which is formed by alpha linkage of galactose to lactosylceramide. The reaction is catalyzed by enzyme A4GALT.[Wiki:Globotriaosylceramide] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Gb3, also known as the Pk antigen, is a type of glycosphingolipid containing a trisaccharide core structure with the sequence Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc. It acts as a receptor for Shiga toxins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(a1-4)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>

 - Pyelonephritis (Source: 27) <br>
 - Acute cystitis (Source: 27) <br>
 - Shigellosis (Source: 27) <br>
 - Lyme disease (Source: 27) <br>
 - Cutaneous lesions (Source: 27) <br>
 - Diarrhea (Source: 27) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=Interaction%20between%20prototype,Pk%20and%20P%20antigens%20%28Chapter%2014%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=This%20lectin%20domain%20was%20found%20to,it%20with%20other%20functional%20domains (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=Interaction%20between%20prototype,Pk%20and%20P%20antigens%20%28Chapter%2014%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Defects in the gene encoding α-galactosidase A lead to accumulation of globotriaosylceramide (GL3) in various cell types.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/32127409 32127409]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== Gb4 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Gb4 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000088 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00061MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00061MO G00061MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00061MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GalNAcβ1-3Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Gb4 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Gb4Cer (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | globoside-4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | globotetraosylceramide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : cytolipin K (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) | globoside (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) | globoside I (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) | P antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An amino tetrasaccharide consisting of 2-acetamido-2-deoxy-β-D-galactopyranosyl, α-D-galactopyranosyl, β-D-galactopyranosyl, and β-D-glucopyranose units joined together in sequence by (1→3), (1→4), and (1→4) glycosidic linkages, respectively.[CHEBI:145578] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Gb4, also known as globoside, is a type of glycosphingolipid containing a tetrasaccharide core structure with the sequence GalNAcβ1-3Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc, found in the P antigen (Gb4) glycosphingolipid. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== globo-series glycospingolipids ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : globo-series glycospingolipids <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000087 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00061MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00061MO G00061MO] <br>

 '''Exact Synonyms''' : globo-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | globosides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | globosides (globo-series) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Globo-series glycosphingolipids are a class of glycosphingolipids characterized by terminal α-galactose residues. They are the most abundant glycolipids in erythrocytes and are degraded by the enzyme α-galactosidase. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=GSL%20series%20are%20expressed%20in,ganglio-series%20neutral%20sugar%20core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch44/#:~:text=Loss%20of%20this%20enzyme%20produces,glycolipids%20close%20to%20the%20membrane (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Results of TLC immunostaining of neutral glycolipids from the cDNA-transfected cells also supported the identity of the newly synthesized component as globoside. [PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10993897 10993897]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== glucosylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glucosylceramide <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000090 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G71142DF <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G71142DF G71142DF] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G71142DF View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Ceramide monohexoside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | CMH (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Glc-Cer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | GlcCer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | glucoceramide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Glucocerebroside (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Cerebrosides which contain as their polar head group a glucose moiety bound in glycosidic linkage to the hydroxyl group of ceramides. Their accumulation in tissue, due to a defect in beta-glucosidase, is the cause of Gaucher's disease.[MeSH:D005963] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glucosylceramide (Glc-Cer) is a glycosphingolipid composed of a ceramide backbone with a single glucose residue attached through a β-glycosidic linkage. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The structural characterization of mixed membranes made of cationic lipids and glucosylceramides may be important for developing novel immunotherapeutic tools such as vaccine adjuvants. (Source: 30611744) <br>
 - Glucosylceramide (GlcCer), the initial GSL synthesized from ceramide by GCS (GlcCer synthase), is required for embryonic survival, but its role in the lung is unknown. (Source: 31265321) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Gaucher's Disease (Source: unknown) <br>

=== glycosylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glycosylceramide <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycosylceramides are glycolipids consisting of a ceramide backbone linked to a short glycan chain, such as glucosylceramide (Glc-Cer) and galactosylceramide (Gal-Cer). These molecules are commonly found in fungi and play important roles in membrane structure and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch23/#:~:text=fumigatus%29%20or%20additional%20mannose,biosynthesis%20%28Chapter%2012%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== glycosylinositolphosphorylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glycosylinositolphosphorylceramide <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GIPCs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | glycosylinositolphosphorylceramides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycosylinositolphosphorylceramides (GIPCs) are plant- and fungi-specific glycolipids composed of a ceramide backbone linked to a glycosylated inositol phosphate headgroup. They are major components of the plant plasma membrane and are absent in animals. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lactosylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : lactosylceramide <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000116 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G84224TW <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G84224TW G84224TW] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G84224TW View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : CDH (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Galβ1-4Glc-Cer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | LacCer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Lc2 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Glycosphingolipids which contain as their polar head group a lactose moiety bound in glycosidic linkage to the hydroxyl group of ceramide. Their accumulation in tissue, due to a defect in lactosylceramide beta-galactosidase, is the cause of lactosylceramidosis.[MeSH:D007790] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Lactosylceramide (LacCer) is a glycosphingolipid core structure composed of a galactose residue β1-4 linked to glucose, which is further attached to a ceramide moiety. It serves as the most common core structure of vertebrate glycosphingolipids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - mammalian cell receptors for Neisseria gonorrhoeae recognized by Helicobacter pylori (Source: EP0078) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :

 - duodenal ulcers (Source: EP0078) <br>
 - gastric adenocarcinoma (Source: 3) <br>
 - gastric ulcers (Source: EP0078) <br>

 - Actinomycosis (Source: 3) <br>

 - Pharyngitis (Source: 3) <br>
 - Tracheobronchitis (Source: 3) <br>
 - Gonorrhea (Source: 3) <br>

=== lactotriaosylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : lactotriaosylceramide <br>

 '''Definition''' :
 - A linear amino trisaccharide consisting of β-D-glucose at the reducing end having an N-acetyl-β-D-glucosaminyl-(1→3)-β-D-galactosyl group attached at the 4-position.[CHEBI:61758] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''Evidence''' :

=== polyglycosylceramide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : polyglycosylceramide <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Polyglycosylceramides are a class of glycolipids, also known as macroglycolipids, characterized by long glycan chains—often containing poly-N-acetyllactosamine—attached to a ceramide lipid anchor. These molecules can carry blood group antigens such as A, B, and H. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=Both%20of%20these%20integral%20membrane,on%20Type-1%20units%20%28Figure%2014.4%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''Description''' :
 - 6-sulfo-sialyl Lewis X (6-sulfo-SLex) is a sialyl Lewis X (LeX) glycan motif that is further modified by sulfation at the 6-position of the N-acetylglucosamine. As a high-affinity L-selectin ligand (often on mucin-type glycoconjugates), it promotes leukocyte rolling and homing at high endothelial venules and can influence cell–cell interactions in inflammation and cancer.) (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprised of a trisaccharide chain of N-acetyl-α-neuraminic acid, 6-O-sulfo-β-D-glactose and N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine residues linked sequentially (2→3) and (1→4), to the GlcNAC residue of which is also linked (1→3) an α-L-fucose residue.[CHEBI:71558] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - 6′-sulfo-sialyl Lewis X (6′-sulfo-SLex) is a sialyl Lewis X (SLex) glycan motif that is further modified by sulfation at the 6′ position of the galactose residue. It serves as a recognition ligand for selectins and Siglecs, guiding cell to cell interactions in the immune system and shaping inflammatory responses at mucosal surfaces. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal6S(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - lymphocyte homing (Source: EP0014) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Influenza (Source: 128) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch35/#:~:text=Apoptosis%20depends%20on%20generation%20of,using%20different%20signaling%20pathways (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Studies using sulfotransferase-deficient mice showed that 6-sulfo sialyl Lewis X (6-sulfo sLe(x)), a major ligand for L-selectin that is expressed on the high endothelial venules (HEVs), plays critical roles in lymphocyte homing to the peripheral lymph nodes.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/2288521 2288521]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== disialyl Lewis a ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : disialyl Lewis a <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000063 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G93400AK <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G93400AK G93400AK] <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Disialyl Lewis is a sialyl Lewis a with an additional sialic acid attached at the C-6 position of penultimate GlcNAc. [PMID:15231659] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycan motif composed of the Lewis a antigen with two sialic acid residues attached. It is present on colonic epithelial cells and its expression is altered in colon cancer. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)[Neu5Ac(a2-6)][Fuc(a1-4)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The 2-3,2-6 disialyl Lea determinant on epithelial cells may play a role in protecting normal epithelial cells. (Source: EP0040) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Mucin antigen carrying 2-3 sialylated Le a antigen or 2-3, 2-6 disialylated Lc4 antigen in cancer patients had the lowest carbohydrate/protein ratio. Thus, the carbohydrate/protein ratio in the type 1 chain mucin antigens in sera of normal subjects is higher than that in sera of cancer patients. (Source: EP0040) <br>

=== Lewis antigens ===

 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Lewis antigens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Lewis blood group antigens (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Lewis type glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Lewis-type structures (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Lewis x ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Lewis x <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000121 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00051MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00051MO G00051MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00051MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galbeta1-4(Fucalpha1-3)GlcNAc (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Le(X) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Le^x (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Lewis x trisaccharide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Lex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | SSEA-1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A Lewis blood group antigen with structure α-L-Fucp-(1→3)-[β-D-Galp-(1→4)]-D-GlcpNAc where the glucosamine at the reducing end has β-configuration at its anomeric centre. Commonly known as Lewis x trisaccharide or Lex.[CHEBI:59294] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Lewis x (Lex) is a fucosylated type 2 chain glycan epitope with the structure Galβ1-4(Fucα1-3)GlcNAc. It is found on N-glycans, O-glycans, and glycolipids in animals and some plants. Glycans containing the Lewis x motif are characterized by the addition of this fucosylated N-acetyllactosamine structure to the glycan chain. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :

=== Lewis y ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Lewis y <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000122 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00052MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00052MO G00052MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00052MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Le(y) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Le^y (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | LeY (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Ley (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A Lewis blood group antigen which is a branched amino tetrasaccharide with structure alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;2)-beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;4)-[alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;3)]-beta-D-GlcpNAc, consisting of consisting of N-acetyl-beta-D-glucosamine at the reducing end having an alpha-L-fucosyl residue attached at the 3-position and an alpha-L-fucosyl-(1→2)-beta-D-galactosyl moiety attached at the 4-position. A cancer-associated tetrasaccharide antigen which constitutes the core structure recognised by therapeutic antibody BR96. Conformational restriction around the GlcNAc residue is a key feature of its structure. It has a role as an antigen and an epitope. [CHEBI:59045]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Fuc(a1-2)Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The expression of the fucosylated histo-blood group antigen CD174 in CD34+ hematopoietic progenitor cells and down-regulation of FUT1 in mature lymphocytes may be important factors influencing the homing process of hematopoietic stem cells to the bone marrow. (Source: EP0018) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - AIDS/acquired immune deficiency syndrome (Source: EP0018) <br>
 - cholangiocarcinoma (Source: EP0018) <br>
 - hepatocellular carcinoma (Source: EP0018) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - In conclusion, Lewis y antigen and CD147 were significantly upregulated in ovarian tumors, and the altered expression of Lewis y may cause changes in CD147.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30816446 30816446]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== sialyl Lewis a ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialyl Lewis a <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000150 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00053MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00053MO G00053MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00053MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : CA19-9 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | CA19-9 antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | sialyl-Lea (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | SLe^a (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | sLea (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | sLeA (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | SLea (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Slea (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : CA19-9 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A sialyated version of Lewis A with a branched structure of amino tetrasaccharide consisting of a sialyl residue, linked (2→3) to a galactosyl residue that in turn is linked (1→3) to a glucosaminyl residue at the reducing end, which is also carrying a fucosyl residue at the 4-position.[CHEBI:62681] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialyl Lewis a (sLea) is a tetrasaccharide glycan motif with the structure Neu5Acα2-3Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAc. It serves as a binding determinant for E-selectin and is recognized as a biomarker in cancer diagnostics, notably detected by the CA19-9 assay. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-3)[Fuc(a1-4)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The carbohydrate determinants,Sialyl Lea and Sialyl Lex,which are frequently expressed on human cancer cells,serve as ligands for a cell adhesion molecule of the selectin family,E-selectin,which is expressed on vascular endothelial cells. These carbohydrate determinants are involved in the adhesion of cancer cells to vascular endothelium and thus contribute to hematogenous metastasis of cancer. (Source: EP0008) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - pancreatic adenocarcinoma (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - hepatocellular cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - cholangiocellular cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - gastric cancer (Source: unknown) <br>
 - [GlycoEpitope:EP0008]colorectal cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - ovarian cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - lung cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - breast cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 - uterine cancer (Source: EP0008) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20a%20critical%20event%20in%20tumor,are%20directed%20by%20O-GlcNAcylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=EMT%20is%20typified%20by%20alterations%20in,used%20in%20monitoring%20ovarian%20cancer (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - By comparing the serum O-glycan profiles from healthy controls with those of cancer patients, we identified a marker candidate, core 1 sialyl Lewis A (NeuAcα2-3Galβ1-3(Fucα1-4)GlcNAcβ1-3Gal) (abbreviated C1SLA), whose concentration appeared to be weakly correlated with CA19-9 values.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/26641888 26641888]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== sialyl Lewis x ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialyl Lewis x <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000151 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00054MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00054MO G00054MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00054MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : sialyl-Lex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | SLe(x) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | SLe^x (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | sLex (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | sLeX (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | SLex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Slex (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A sialylated version of Lewis X antigen expressed on cell surfaces with structure alpha-Neup5Ac-(2-&gt;3)-beta-D-Galp-(1-&gt;4)-[alpha-L-Fucp-(1-&gt;3)]-beta-D-GlcpNAcis α-Neup5Ac-(2→3)-β-D-Galp-(1→4)-[α-L-Fucp-(1→3)]-D-GlcpNAc in which the anomeric configuration of the reducing-end N-acetyl-beta-D-glucosamine residue is beta. It is a ligand for selectins. [MESH:2030970,CHEI:71622] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialyl Lewis x (sLex) is a tetrasaccharide glycan motif with the structure Neu5Acα2-3Galβ1-4(Fucα1-3)GlcNAc. It is a well-known selectin ligand involved in cell adhesion, particularly mediating leukocyte rolling, trafficking, and extravasation during inflammation and immune responses. sLex can be found as a terminal cap on both N- and O-glycans, including core 2 O-glycans, and is used as a biomarker in various biological and pathological processes such as hematopoiesis and leukemogenesis. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Neu5Ac(a2-3)Gal(b1-4)[Fuc(a1-3)]GlcNAc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Sialyl Lex was shown to be a specific ligand on human leukocytes for vascular E- and P-selectins,and was shown to mediate leukocyte recruitment. (Source: EP0012) <br>
 - The determinant is also frequently expressed on human cancer cells,and is involved in the adhesion of cancer cells to vascular endothelium and thus contribute to hematogenous metastasis of cancer. (Source: EP0012) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - colon cancer (Source: EP0012) <br>
 - colorectal cancer (Source: EP0012) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=Some%20of%20these%20changes%20have%20been,consisting%20predominantly%20of%20mucins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== high-mannose N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : high-mannose N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000108 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G55220VL <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G55220VL G55220VL] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G55220VL View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : high mannose glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | high-mannose-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | oligomannose N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | oligomannose, high-mannose, oligomannose-type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | oligomannose-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | oligomannosidic glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | oligomannosyl N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An N-linked oligosaccharide that possesses between five and nine mannose residues attached to the chitobiose [(GlcNAc)2] core. [CHEBI:5717] None of the Man residues are substituted with any monosaccharide other than Man . (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - High-mannose N-glycans, also known as oligomannose-type glycans, are a class of N-linked glycans characterized by a core pentasaccharide (Man3GlcNAc2) that is extended exclusively with additional mannose residues, typically resulting in structures with five to nine mannose units. These glycans are biosynthesized in the endoplasmic reticulum from dolichol-linked precursors and are found on glycoproteins, where they play important roles in protein folding, quality control, trafficking, and recognition. High-mannose N-glycans can serve as ligands for lectins, are substrates for enzymes such as endo H, and may be further modified, for example by phosphorylation during lysosomal targeting. They lack complex antennae and are notable for their strong binding to ConA-agarose. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Involved in promotion of protein folding in ER. [PMID:12417032]. (Source: unknown) <br>
 - High Man glycans are enriched at cell surface during apoptosis. (Source:  17516205) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Relative abundance changes in multiple CDGs [PMID: 30770376] (Source:  28484880) <br>

=== paucimannose N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : paucimannose N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000144 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Man2-3GlcNAc2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | paucimannose (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | paucimannose glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | paucimannose-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | paucimannosidic glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | paucimannosidic N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Any N-linked glycan derivative with composition Man1-4Fuc0-1 GlcNAc2, in which the chitobiose core (GlcNAc2) is intact. Found in relatively large amounts in invertebrates and plants, they are formed by removal of the non-reducing terminal GlcNAc (\added by GlcNAcT1) from GlcNAcMan3-5GlcNAc2 by a Golgi hexosaminidase and subsequent removal of Man residues by Golgi mannosidases. Pauscimannose glycans have also been detected in vertebrates.[CHEBI:144386] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Paucimannose N-glycans are a class of truncated N-linked glycans characterized by a chitobiose core (GlcNAc2) and a reduced number of mannose residues, typically two or three, but sometimes up to five (e.g., Man2–5GlcNAc2). These structures often lack sialic acids and other anionic groups, and may be modified by core α1-6-fucosylation or other substitutions. Paucimannose N-glycans arise from extensive trimming of high-mannose N-glycans during glycan processing, involving the action of Golgi β-hexosaminidases and α-mannosidase, resulting in shorter glycan chains compared to complex or oligomannose N-glycans. They are commonly found in insects, plants, some invertebrates, and occasionally in mammals. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=In%20terms%20of%20Golgi%20modifications%20of,and%20the%20Co%2B%2B-dependent%20AMAN-3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=GlcNAcT-I%20catalyzes%20the%20first,have%20not%20been%20characterized (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Brain%20lobes%2C%20normally%20separated%20in,activity%20and%20decreased%20life%20span (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-GalNAc core structures ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-GalNAc core structures <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : cores 1–4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | O-GalNAc cores (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-GalNAc core structures are conserved oligosaccharide cores formed by the extension of the initial O-linked GalNAc residue on serine or threonine. These structures, known as cores 1–4, serve as the foundational building blocks for complex O-GalNAc glycans, particularly in mucins, and are essential for O-glycan biosynthesis in vertebrates. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-GalNAc-linked sialoglycans ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-GalNAc-linked sialoglycans <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-glycans ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-glycans <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000139 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-linked oligosaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Ser/Thr-linked glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - The carbohydrate portion of a glycoprotein that has a glycan linked through the hydroxyl oxygen of serine, threonine, hydroxylysine or hydroxyproline side-chains.[CHEBI:59521] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-glycans are a class of glycans covalently attached to the serine or threonine residues of proteins via O-glycosidic bonds. Common in animals, they display diverse core structures often containing GalNAc, GlcNAc, Gal, fucose, and sialic acid. O-glycans are synthesized in the Golgi apparatus and play important roles in protein function, stability, and cell-cell interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Affects several important activities like protein stability, modulation of signaling molecules and enzyme activity. (Source: 9673446) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

=== O-linked fucose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-linked fucose <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-linked fucose refers to a glycan modification in which a fucose residue is attached via an O-linkage to serine or threonine residues of proteins. This modification is particularly important in modulating developmental signaling pathways, such as those involving the Notch receptor. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Rt%20and%20Tw%20proteins%20must%20be,O-fut1%20and%20O-fut2 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Elongation%20of%20O-linked%20fucose%20with,with%20sialic%20acid%20%28Chapter%2013%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-linked Glc-Gal disaccharide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-linked Glc-Gal disaccharide <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-Linked Glc-Gal (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A disaccharide composed of glucose and galactose that is O-linked to hydroxylysine residues, playing an important role in collagen fibril formation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-linked glucose <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :

=== O-linked mannose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-linked mannose <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycan modification in which a mannose residue is attached via an O-linkage to serine or threonine residues of proteins. This modification is conserved across species and is implicated in muscular dystrophies. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-mannose glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-mannose glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000140 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G59126YU <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G59126YU G59126YU] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G59126YU View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-Man glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | O-mannose initiated glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | O-mannose-based glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | O-mannosyl glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - O-linked glycans that are initiated by the addtion of a Man residue to Ser or Thr. Subsequent extension of the Man defines the O-Man core type, either M1, M2, or M3. The M1 type is initated by addition of a GlcNAc in β-linkage to the 2 position of the Man residue. M2 is initiated by addition of another GlcNAc to the 6 position of the M1 core. M3 is initiated by the addition of a GlcNAc in β-linkage to the 4 position of the Man residue. M1 and M2 type core glycans can be extended and branched with Gal, Fuc, Sialic Acid, and GlcA. The M3 core is uniquely extended by a multienzyme system to generate the matriglycan polysaccharide, a protein-specific modification found only on alpha-dystroglycan. [CHEBI:87761] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-mannose glycans are a class of O-glycans initiated by the attachment of mannose to serine or threonine residues of proteins. Found in mammals, yeast, and other organisms, these glycans can be extended into several core structures, such as core M1 and M2. They play important roles in protein function, cell signaling, and act as receptors for viruses and extracellular matrix proteins. In mammals, O-mannose glycans are especially abundant in the brain and on α-dystroglycan, with defects in their biosynthesis linked to congenital muscular dystrophies. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - MEB/muscle-eye-brain disease (Source: EP0002) <br>
 - WWS/walker-warburg syndrome (Source: unknown) <br>
 - muscular dystrophy (Source: EP0002) <br>
 - neuronal migration disorder (Source: EP0002) <br>
 - Muscle-Eye-Brain disease (MEB) and Walker-Warburg Syndrome (WWS) have mutations in genes encoding glycosyltransferases needed for O -mannosyl oligosaccharide synthesis (Source: EP0002) <br>
 - Muscular dystrophy and neuronal migration disorder are caused by mutations in a glycosyltransferase, POMGnT1. (Source: EP0002) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=NGLY1%20defects%20do%20not%20appear%20to,results%20in%20muscular%20dystrophy (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=The%20pathway%20is%20initiated%20in%20the%20ER,O-Man%20glycans%20are%20shown (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=O-Man%20glycan%20biosynthetic%20pathway.,DPM2%2C%20and%20DPM3%20for%20Dol-P-Man%5D%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=The%20many%20CHO%20mutants%20have%20recently,these%20glycans%20to%20be%20truncated (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=Glycosylation%20mutants%20of%20mammalian,in%20the%20context%20of%20a%20living%20cell (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== O-xylose-linked glycosaminoglycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-xylose-linked glycosaminoglycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-xylose-linked GAG chains (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - A glycosaminoglycan (GAG) chain that is covalently attached to a core protein through an O-linked xylose residue. === 2-6-branch of complex N-glycans ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : 2-6-branch of complex N-glycans <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : 2-6-branch (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The 2-6-branch of complex N-glycans refers to the α1-6 core mannose that is substituted at both the 2 and 6 positions, bearing GlcNAcβ1-6 and GlcNAcβ1-2 antennae. This structure typically features a Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-2(Galβ1-4GlcNAcβ1-6)Manα1-R motif, representing a common branching pattern in complex N-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=A%20good%20example%20of%20this%20is%20the,%28MGAT5%20and%20-5B%29%20%28Chapter%209%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== 2′-fucosyllactose ===

 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - pancreas cancer (Source: EP0064) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

=== a-series gangliosides ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : a-series gangliosides <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - a-series gangliosides are a class of glycosphingolipids characterized by the presence of a single sialic acid residue attached to the inner galactose of the lactosylceramide core. They are biosynthesized through the addition of sialic acid to GM3 and cannot be directly converted to b-series gangliosides by sialyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Each%20branch%20is%20a%20committed,by%20a%20single%20galactosyltransferase (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== alkylacylphosphatidylinositol-anchored glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : alkylacylphosphatidylinositol-anchored glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycan structure that is attached to a membrane through an alkylacylphosphatidylinositol anchor. This glycan may contain components such as nonacetylated glucosamine, mannose, galactofuranose, and 2-aminoethylphosphonate. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== arthroseries glycosphingolipids ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : arthroseries glycosphingolipids <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : arthro-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | arthroseries (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | arthroseries glycolipids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A family of glycosphingolipids (GSLs) characteristic of invertebrates, especially arthropods, built on a mactosylceramide core (Manβ1-4Glcβ-ceramide) that may contain multiple N-acetylglucosamine residues and is often further modified with galactose, fucose, glucuronic acid, phosphoethanolamine, or phosphorylcholine. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=In%20skin%2C%20it%20is%20a%20precursor%20of,in%20tissue-specific%20patterns (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=At%20least%20three%20major%20classes%20of,insect%20pests.%20Bt%20is%20toxic%20to%20C (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=dentatum%20%28Figure%2025.8%29.%20Examples,of%20mammalian%20innate%20immune%20systems (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=However%2C%20arthropods%20possess%20their,core%20%28Gal%CE%B21-4Glc%CE%B2-ceramide%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=The%20functional%20significance%20of,as%20modulators%20of%20cell%20fate (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== asialo-GM1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : asialo-GM1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000019 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G21856LC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G21856LC G21856LC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G21856LC View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : aGM1 (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) | as-GM1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Asialo-GM1a (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | GA-1 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | ganglio-N-tetraosylceramide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Gangliotetraosylceramide (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Gg4 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | GgO4 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : GA1 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Asialo-GM1, also known as GA1, is a neutral ganglioside of the ganglio-series with the structure Galβ1-3GalNAcβ1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer. It is formed by extending lactosylceramide with GalNAc (β1-4) followed by Gal (β1-3). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(b1-4)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :

 - Porcine fibrinohemorrhagic necrotizing pleuropneumonia (Source: 2) <br>
 - Actinomycosis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Lazy leukocyte syndrome (Source: 2) <br>
 - Pertussis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Cutaneous lesions (Source: 2) <br>
 - Melioidosis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Candidiasis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Taiwan acute respiratory agent (Source: 2) <br>
 - Trachoma (Source: 2) <br>
 - Cystitis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Chronic gastritis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Peptic ulcers (Source: 2) <br>
 - Gastric cancer (Source: 2) <br>
 - Klebsiella pneumonia infection (Source: 2) <br>
 - Pneumonia (Source: 2) <br>
 - Legionnaires' disease (Source: 2) <br>
 - Gonorrhea (Source: 2) <br>
 - Gastroenteritis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Toxoplasmosis (Source: 2) <br>
 - Plague (Source: 2) <br>
 - Diarrhea (Source: 2) <br>

=== b-series gangliosides ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : b-series gangliosides <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : b-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - b-series gangliosides are a class of glycosphingolipids characterized by the presence of two sialic acid residues attached to the inner galactose. Examples include GD3 and GT1b. These structures are formed through the action of sialyltransferases during glycosphingolipid biosynthesis. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== biantennary complex-type N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : biantennary complex-type N-glycan <br>

 '''Definition''' :
 - A complex N-glycan that has two branches attached to the non-reducing terminal Man residues of the trimannosyl core. . Each branch is initiated with a GlcNAc and can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. [CHEBI:156251] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - N-linked glycans with a complex-type structure characterized by two antennae (branches) extending from the core. These glycans are commonly found on glycoproteins and are known for their specific lectin binding properties. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== biantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : biantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000022 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : diantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Type of N-linked glycan in which two antenna are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). Each antenna can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Biantennary N-glycans are N-linked glycans characterized by two antennae (branches) extending from the core pentasaccharide structure. These branches can be further modified by the addition of sugars such as galactose, sialic acid, or fucose. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== bisected complex-type N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : bisected complex-type N-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A complex-type N-glycan structure that contains a bisecting N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) residue attached via β1-4 linkage to the core mannose of N-glycans. This modification is typically introduced by the enzyme MGAT3. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
