=== bisecting GlcNAc ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : bisecting GlcNAc <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000024 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : bisecting N-acetylglucosamine (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | β1-4-GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - The bisecting GlcNAc is a modification of hybrid or complex N-linked glycans in which a single GlcNAc is added to the 3 position of the b-linked Man found in the trimannosyl core structure. The modification is catalyzed by beta 1,4-N-acetylglucosaminyltransferase III (GlcNAcT-III) or MGAT3 (E.C. 2.4.1.144). The addition of a bisecting GlcNAc on a hybrid or trimmed hybrid glycan prevents the subsequent action of N-glycan branching glycosyltransferases GlcNAcT-IV and GlcNAcT-V, but GlcNAcT3 can add a bisecting GlcNAc to the products of these enzymes. [PMID: 22476631] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A β1-4-linked N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) residue attached to the core mannose of N-glycans, formed by the action of N-acetylglucosaminyltransferase III (MGAT3). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - The bisecting GlcNAc of N-glycans regulates cellular signaling and tumor progression through modulating N-glycan/galectin interactions.[PMID: 22476631]Inhibits growth factor signaling and retards mammary tumor progression[PMID: 20395209]Integrin-mediated cell adhesion [PMID: 20816221]Important in fertilization and fetal development, neuritogenesis, immune tolerance, immunoglobulin G (IgG) (Source:  32719771) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - mammary tumor. (Source:  20395209) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Increased%20expression%20of%20FUT8%20that,depend%20on%20tissue%20context (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch28/#:~:text=It%20is%20also%20noteworthy%20that%20viruses,evolved%20by%20convergent%20evolution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - The presence of a bisecting GlcNAc and the occurrence of alpha 2-6-linked Neu5Ac in the most abundant N-glycans, are new features for hCG-beta.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1374031 1374031]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Publications''' : 51 references <br>

=== CD77 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : CD77 <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : Gb3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Cluster of Differentiation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - CD77 is a neutral glycosphingolipid consisting of Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer, also known as globotriaosylceramide (Gb3). It serves as a tumor-associated carbohydrate antigen, being highly enriched in certain cancers such as Burkitt’s lymphoma, and is recognized by specific monoclonal antibodies. As a component of lipid raft microdomains, CD77 influences cell signaling and can modulate tumor cell growth and invasion. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Many%20%E2%80%9Ctumor-specific%E2%80%9D%20monoclonal,signaling%20by%20numerous%20receptors (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== chitobiose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : chitobiose <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : chito-motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Chitobiose is a disaccharide composed of two β(1→4)-linked N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) units, forming the core structure of N-glycans. In some biological contexts, this chitobiose core can be elongated by additional monosaccharides, particularly in cases involving defects in mannosyltransferase activity or in certain parasitic organisms, resulting in extended chitobiose motifs. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== cisGM1 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : cisGM1 <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000044 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G03277YI <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G03277YI G03277YI] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G03277YI View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GM1b (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : GM1b (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A member of the class of neuraminic acids that is neuraminic acid attached in sequence to β-D-galactopyranosyl, 2-acetamido-2-deoxy-β-D-galactopyranosyl, β-D-galactopyranosyl, and β-D-glucoopyranose units by (2→3), (1→3), (1→4), and (1→4) glycosidic linkages, respectively. It corresponds to the carbohydrate portion of ganglioside GM1b.[CHEBI:145580] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Influenza (Source: 92) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - Macrophage ganglioside patterns dramatically increase in complexity when murine peritoneal macrophages are stimulated in vivo with the appearance of the sialidase-sensitive monosialoganglioside GM1b (cisGM1) as a major component.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/9455923 9455923]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== complex ganglioside ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : complex ganglioside <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex gangliosides are gangliosides with multiple glycan extensions beyond the simple monosialylated core, often containing additional sialic acid and N-acetylgalactosamine residues. They are synthesized by the sequential action of sialyltransferases and N-acetylgalactosaminyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch45/#:~:text=The%20patients%E2%80%99%20plasma%20GSLs%20are,the%20progressive%20gait%20disorder (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== complex glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : complex glycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : complex oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex glycans are oligosaccharides with branched and extended structures, typically referring to mature N-glycans or O-glycans that have undergone extensive processing and addition of various sugars beyond the core. These structures often feature complex sugar chains, such as sialylated, sulfated, glucuronylated, or zwitterionic modifications, and include both complex N-glycans and other complex-type glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Consumption%20of%20these%20plants%20by,vs.%20%CE%B1-mannosidase%29%20does%20not%20exist (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Although%20it%20was%20once%20thought%20that,in%20Drosophila%20and%20other%20insects (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=GlcNAcT-I%20catalyzes%20the%20first,have%20not%20been%20characterized (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== complex glycolipid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : complex glycolipid <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex glycolipids are glycolipids that possess oligosaccharide chains longer or more branched than those found in simple mono- or disaccharide glycolipids. They are synthesized from precursors like glucosylceramide through additional glycosylation steps. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch55/#:~:text=Because%20cells%20take%20up,is%20missing%20%28Chapter%2044%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== complex O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : complex O-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex O-glycans are O-glycans with extended and branched structures, typically formed by the sequential addition of monosaccharides to an initial N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) linked to serine or threonine residues of proteins. These glycans are highly diverse and are commonly found on mucins and other glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch08/#:~:text=The%20baculovirus%20enzyme%20ecdysteroid,in%20their%20symbiosis%20and%20virulence (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== complex-type N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : complex-type N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000045 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : complex N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | complex N-linked glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | complex-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Type of N-linked glycan in which at least two antenna are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). Each antenna can be exteneded or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex-type N-glycans are a class of N-linked glycans characterized by the addition of N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) to the core mannose residues, followed by further branching and elongation with sugars such as galactose, sialic acid, and fucose. These glycans are typically multiantennary and highly branched, formed after mannose trimming in the Golgi apparatus and subsequent action of glycosyltransferases. Their biosynthesis involves full processing of the GlcNAcMan5GlcNAc2 intermediate, and they are commonly found on glycoproteins in higher eukaryotes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Involved in protein stability. (Source: 12417032) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=Paucimannose%20glycans%20are%20also,GlcNAc-TIII%20%28MGAT3%29%20%28Figure%209.5%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=Similarly%2C%20one%20of%20the%20first,cause%20an%20obvious%20growth%20phenotype (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=Complex%20and%20hybrid-type%20N-glycans,the%20mammalian%20immune%20system (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=Deletion%20of%20both%20%CE%B1-mannosidases,lack%20the%20same%20enzyme%20%28Chapter%2045%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch30/#:~:text=Ideally%2C%20the%20saturation%20effect,of%20the%20virus%20to%20target%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Forssman glycolipid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Forssman glycolipid <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000069 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G80062GG <br>

 '''Definition''' :
 - A linear amino pentasaccharide comprising N-acetyl-α-D-galactosamine, N-acetyl-β-D-galactosamine, α-D-galactose, β-D-galactose and β-D-glucose residues linked sequentially (1→3), (1→3), (1→4) and (1→4).[CHEBI:85789] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 - <code>GalNAc(a1-3)GalNAc(b1-3)Gal(a1-4)Gal(b1-4)Glc(b1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - an antigenic marker for a major subpopulation of macrophages from murine spleen and peripheral lymph nodes (Source: 3495590) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Pyelonephritis (Source: 86) <br>
 - Acute cystitis (Source: 86) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - Analysis of metabolically labeled glycolipids released into the apical and basal culture medium, either as shed membrane vesicles or in budding viruses, also demonstrated the presence of the Forssman glycolipid on both apical and basolateral membranes of polarized cells.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/3040119 3040119]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== galabiose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : galabiose <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galα1-4Gal determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Galα1-4Gal moiety (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Galabiose is a disaccharide composed of two galactose residues linked by an alpha1-4 glycosidic bond (Galα1-4Gal). It serves as a recognition motif for certain bacterial toxins and adhesins, and is found in glycolipids and glycoproteins, including as part of glycosphingolipids where it can be linked to ceramide. Galabiose-containing structures are present in the large intestine epithelium and function as receptors for specific pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== ganglio-series glycosphingolipid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : ganglio-series glycosphingolipid <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : ganglio-series (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Ganglio-series glycosphingolipids are a class of glycosphingolipids defined by a specific core oligosaccharide structure (Galβ1-3GalNAcβ1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer), which can be further extended and sialylated to form gangliosides. These molecules are predominant in the mammalian brain and are classified based on the number of sialic acid residues and their migration order on thin-layer chromatography, such as GM (mono), GD (di), and GT (tri) series. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch54/#:~:text=Glycosyltransferase-mediated,was%20employed%20twice%20%28Figure%2054.3%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== ganglioside ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : ganglioside <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Gangliosides are glycosphingolipids (glycolipids) characterized by one or more sialic acid residues linked to an oligosaccharide chain, which is attached to a ceramide lipid. As a subclass of sialylated glycosphingolipids, they are abundant in the nervous system, particularly the brain, and function as important cell surface molecules involved in cell signaling, recognition, and as receptors for toxins and viruses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Glc3Man9GlcNAc2 ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Glc3Man9GlcNAc2 <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Composition (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glc3Man9GlcNAc2 is a fully assembled N-glycan precursor oligosaccharide consisting of three glucose, nine mannose, and two N-acetylglucosamine residues. It is transferred en bloc to nascent proteins, covalently linked to asparagine, during N-glycosylation in eukaryotes, representing the initial glycan attached to glycoproteins before further processing in the endoplasmic reticulum and Golgi apparatus. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''term (main_entry)''' : GlcNAcβ1-4Man <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : IUPAC String (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A core disaccharide structure found in complex-type N-glycans, consisting of N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) β1-4 linked to mannose (Man). This motif is produced by the enzyme MGAT3 and is recognized by the E-PHA lectin. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch48/#:~:text=L-PHA%20binding%20is%20dramatically,samples%20differ%20in%20glycosylation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Glcα1-phosphate-Man ===

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Disaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Glcβ1-3Fucα-O- ===

 '''Description''' :
 - A disaccharide modification found on O-fucosylated serine or threonine residues, where a glucose (Glc) is β1-3 linked to a fucose (Fuc) that is α-linked to serine or threonine. This structure is commonly present on thrombospondin type 1 repeats (TSRs). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== glycosphingolipids ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glycosphingolipids <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GSLs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycosphingolipids (GSLs) are a subclass of glycolipids composed of a glycan (oligosaccharide) linked to a ceramide lipid moiety. They are major components of animal cell membranes, synthesized by the stepwise addition of sugars to ceramide, and are classified based on their glycan structures. GSLs play important roles in membrane structure, cell–cell recognition, and signal transduction. They are degraded in lysosomes, and defects in their catabolism can result in pathological accumulation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Ronald%20L.%20Schnaar%2C%20Roger%20Sandhoff%2C,as%20membrane%20anchors%20%28Chapter%2012%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20in%20the%20widely%20used,as%20examples%20are%20major%20brain%20GSLs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch11/#:~:text=Glycosphingolipids%20%28GSLs%29%20are,and%20adsorption%20chromatographies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch25/#:~:text=elegans%20is%20similar%20to%20the%20chains,%28GSLs%29%20%28Chapter%2011%29%20in%20C (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== GPI glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : GPI glycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : glycosylphosphatidylinositol (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | GPI anchor (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Manα1-2Manα1-6Manα1-4GlcNα1-6Inositolphospholipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - GPI glycans are the glycan (oligosaccharide) portion of glycosylphosphatidylinositol (GPI) anchors, which are glycolipid structures that tether proteins to the outer leaflet of the plasma membrane. The core GPI glycan consists of a conserved sequence, typically including mannose and glucosamine residues linked to myo-inositol phospholipid, and serves as a scaffold for further modifications and protein attachment. GPI glycans can vary between species and tissues, contributing to the diversity of GPI-anchored proteins, and may include additional side chains. In some organisms, the lipid component is inositolphosphoceramide. The GPI glycan is preassembled before protein attachment and is essential for the function of GPI anchors. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20primary%20control%20occurs%20at,significance%20remains%20unknown (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Once%20labeled%20and%20dephosphorylated,of%20a%20cell-free%20system%20in%20T.%20brucei (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch49/#:~:text=Loss%20of%20GPI-anchor%20biosynthesis,of%20the%20GPI%20anchor%20to%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=For%20example%2C%20neural%20cell%20adhesion,proteoglycans%29%20moiety%20%28Chapter%2017%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch12/#:~:text=Sneha%20Sudha%20Komath%2C%20Morihisa,type-I%20integral%20membrane%20proteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== hybrid N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : hybrid N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000110 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G16828VN <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G16828VN G16828VN] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G16828VN View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : hybrid-type glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | hybrid-type N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An N-linked glycan in which the Manα1-6 arm of the trimannosyl core Manα1-3(Manα1-6)Manβ1-4GlcNAcβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–Asn-X-Ser/Thr is substituted by one or more additional Man residues and the Manα1-3 arm is substituted by a GlcNAc residue added by GlcNAcT1. The GlcNAc linked to the a3 Man can be extended in the same manner as any complex glycan antenna. The trimannosyl core can also be core fucosylated and/or bisected by the addition of a GlcNAc to the 3 position of the α-linked Man residue.[Essentials of Glycobiology:Chapter 9] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Hybrid N-glycans are a class of N-linked glycans that display features of both oligomannose and complex N-glycans. They are characterized by having one branch (antenna) that retains mannose residues, while the other branch is extended with N-acetylglucosamine and other complex sugars. These structures are biosynthesized when the GlcNAcMan5GlcNAc2 intermediate produced by MGAT1 is not fully processed by α-mannosidase II, resulting in glycans that are intermediates between oligomannose and complex N-glycans. Hybrid N-glycans can be further modified in the Golgi apparatus. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch27/#:~:text=In%20most%20cases%2C%20these%20mutations,of%20hybrid%20and%20complex%20N-glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch44/#:~:text=Cathepsin%20A%20protects,hybrid%20and%20complex-type%20N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch09/#:~:text=It%20is%20important%20to%20note%20that,and%20stem%20cell%20development (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch24/#:~:text=In%20the%20trans-Golgi%2C%20%CE%B1-Fucf%20may%20be,or%20transported%20to%20the%20vacuoles (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch29/#:~:text=Such%20an%20approach%20has%20been,N-glycans%20will%20not%20bind (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lipid A core oligosaccharide ===

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The lipid A-core is a glycan-lipid structure in which lipid A is covalently linked to the core oligosaccharide region of lipopolysaccharide (LPS). This structure serves as a substrate for transport and further modification in Gram-negative bacteria. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lipo-chitooligosaccharide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : lipo-chitooligosaccharide <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : LCO (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | lipochitooligosaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Lipo-chitooligosaccharides (LCOs) are a class of glycolipids consisting of a chitin-derived oligosaccharide backbone linked to a fatty acid. They are produced by bacterial and fungal symbionts and are recognized by plant receptors, triggering various biological responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lipoglycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : lipoglycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Lipoglycans are a broad class of glycoconjugates in which a glycan, such as an oligosaccharide or polysaccharide, is covalently linked to a lipid moiety. These molecules contribute to cell membrane structure and function. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lipopolysaccharide ===

=== M7BC ===

 '''Description''' :
 - M7BC is a specific isomer of the Man7 (M7) high-mannose N-glycan. It is characterized by the absence of residues i and k compared to M8B, resulting in the exposure of an α1–6-linked mannose (residue j). === monoantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : monoantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A monoantennary N-glycan is an N-glycan structure characterized by having a single antenna (branch) extending from the core. This type of glycan is often produced in engineered CHO cells, is typically unsialylated, and features exposed galactose acceptor sites. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''Description''' :
 - A high-mannose-type N-glycan containing a single glucose residue. This structure is recognized by calnexin and calreticulin in the endoplasmic reticulum and plays a key role in the glycoprotein folding quality control system. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''Description''' :

=== N-glycan core ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : N-glycan core <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The N-glycan core is the conserved core structure (Manα1–6(Manα1–3)Manβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–4GlcNAcβ1–Asn) found in all N-linked glycans found on eukaryotic proteins. It is assembled during the process of N-glycosylation and is essential for the proper formation and function of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=To%20date%2C%20relatively%20few%20mutations,fused%20lobes%20%28Figure%2026.2%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== N-glycans ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : N-glycans <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000131 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G78059CC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G78059CC G78059CC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G78059CC View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : asparagine–N-linked glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | N-linked (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | N-linked oligosaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - The carbohydrate portion of a glycoprotein that has a glycan linked through the nitrogen of an asparagine side-chain.[CHEBI:59520] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - N-glycans are a class of glycans covalently attached to the asparagine residues of proteins via N-glycosidic bonds. They are characterized by a conserved core pentasaccharide structure and are commonly found in animals, but the N-glycosylation system is present across all domains of life. N-glycans are synthesized in the endoplasmic reticulum and Golgi apparatus, and their structures can be further elaborated with various monosaccharides such as GlcNAc, GalNAc, Gal, Man, Fuc, and sialic acid. These glycans play important roles in protein folding, stability, and cell signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - N-glycans modulate the function of several cell surface proteins which involved in migration, adhesion and which are responsible for regulating myelination. (Source:  25151374) <br>
 - Affect transportation of the glycosylated proteins in the Golgi. (Source:  25151374) <br>
 - Affects cell-cell interaction, adhesion, expression, proper folding. (Source:  25151374) <br>
 - extracellular N-glycans act as key modulators of neuronal uptake of αacetyl [PMID: 31211781]. (Source: unknown) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Rheumatoid arthritis (Source: 17392038) <br>
 - Type 1 Diabetes (Source: 29146600) <br>
 - Lung Cancer (Source:  23766031) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch06/#:~:text=The%20glycosyltransferases%20that,regions%20of%20the%20folded%20protein (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=In%20this%20regard%2C%20whereas,and%20some%20memory/effector%20T%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch54/#:~:text=By%20using%20this%20method%20with%20various,galactose%20residue%20%28Figure%2054.4%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch52/#:~:text=The%20Australian%20company%2C%20Proteome,and%20in%20cell%E2%80%93cell%20communication (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch01/#:~:text=Remarkably%20little%20is%20still%20known,microorganisms%20and%20various%20toxins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== nonfucosylated triantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : nonfucosylated triantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000135 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A triantennary N-linked glycan with three GlcNAc branches linked to the core and no fucose appears. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== sialoglycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialoglycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Sia-containing glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | sialic acid-containing glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialoglycans are glycans (oligosaccharides or polysaccharides) that contain one or more sialic acid residues, typically as terminal (outermost) sugars on structures such as N-glycans, O-glycans, glycosphingolipids, milk oligosaccharides, or glycosylphosphatidylinositol (GPI) anchors. These molecules play important roles in cell recognition, immune modulation, and interactions with pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Siaα2-3Galβ1-4GlcNAc ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Siaα2-3Galβ1-4GlcNAc <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : IUPAC String (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A trisaccharide glycan structure where sialic acid (Sia) is α2-3 linked to galactose (Gal), which is β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc). This structure can be formed by elongation of O-GlcNAc with β1,4Gal followed by sialylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== terminal N-acetylglucosamine ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : terminal N-acetylglucosamine <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : exposed GlcNAc residues (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | terminal GlcNAc residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== terminal sialic acid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : terminal sialic acid <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Terminal Sia residue (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== terminal β-linked N-acetylgalactosamine ===

=== tetraantennary complex-type N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : tetraantennary complex-type N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000165 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Type of N-linked glycan in which four antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The four antennae are initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 2 GlcNAcs to the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions) of the trimannosyl core. Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - A variety of high-mannose structures , as well as bi-, tri-, and tetraantennary complex-type carbohydrate , has been found, in relative amounts of 1-25% of the total oligosaccharides.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1899031 1899031]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== tetraantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : tetraantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000164 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Type of N-linked glycan in which four antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The four antennae are initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 2 GlcNAcs to the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions) of the trimannosyl core. Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A tetraantennary N-glycan is an N-linked glycan structure characterized by four antennae (branches) extending from the core. These branches often terminate in β-linked galactose (Gal) or N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues and are frequently associated with increased sialylation. Tetra-antennary N-glycans are notable for their enhanced biological activity, such as in erythropoietin (EPO), and can exhibit high-affinity binding to certain receptors, including the rat AMR. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch57/#:~:text=Erythropoietin%20%28EPO%29%20is%20the%20most,activity%20in%20vivo%20nearly%20tenfold (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20efficiency%20of%20the%20AMR%20in,normal%20without%20challenge (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - In contrast, myeloma IgE showed a higher abundance of triantennary and tetraantennary glycan structures and a low abundance of species with a bisecting N-acetylglucosamine.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24308486 24308486]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== trehalose-6,6-dimycolate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : trehalose-6,6-dimycolate <br>

=== triantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : triantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000170 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : tri-antennary N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Type of N-linked glycan in which three antennae are present on the trimannosyl core, each initiated by GlcNAc additions catalyzed by position-specific N-acetylglucosaminyltransferases (GlcNAcTs). The three antennae can be be initiated by addition of 2 GlcNAcs to the _3 Man (at the 2 and 4 positions) and 1 GlcNAc to the _6 Man (at the 2 position) of the trimannosyl core or 1 on the _3 and 2 on the _6 Man (at the 2 and 6 positions). Each antenna can be extended or capped with HexNAc, deoxyHex, Hex, or sialic acid residues.  [Essentials of Glycobiology:Glossary] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A triantennary N-glycan is an N-linked glycan structure featuring three antennae (branches) extending from the core. These branches often terminate in β-linked galactose (Gal) or N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residues. Triantennary N-glycans are recognized for their high-affinity binding to the rat asialoglycoprotein receptor (AMR). (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch34/#:~:text=The%20efficiency%20of%20the%20AMR%20in,normal%20without%20challenge (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Increased expression of the high-mannose M6N2 and NeuAc3H3N3M3N2F tri-antennary N-glycans in cholangiocarcinoma.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/29399163 29399163]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== trimannosyl core ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : trimannosyl core <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000175 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A glycan that constitutes the core structure common to all high-mannose, complex, or hybrid N-glycans. The trimannosyl core composition is Man3 GlcNAc2 and includes a chitobiose at the reducing terminal which is extended by a single b-linked Man residue. The b-linked Man is branched by two additional a-linked Man residues, one at the 3 position and one at the 6 position. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - This glycoengineered H. polymorpha strain produced glycoproteins mainly containing trimannosyl core N-glycan (Man(3)GlcNAc(2)), which is the common core backbone of various human-type N-glycans.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/18320567 18320567]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== truncated core 1 O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : truncated core 1 O-glycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : truncated core 1 structure (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Truncated core 1 O-glycans are O-glycans based on the core 1 structure (Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) that are incompletely extended. Examples include the Tn antigen (GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr) and the T antigen (Galβ1-3GalNAcα1-Ser/Thr), which represent early intermediates or incomplete forms of core 1 O-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Like%20plants%2C%20some%20insect%20cells%20may,core%201%20structures%20%28Tn%20and%20T%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== truncated N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : truncated N-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Truncated N-glycans are N-linked glycans that are incompletely processed, often due to prolonged retention in the endoplasmic reticulum, resulting in shorter glycan chains than typically found in mature N-glycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch39/#:~:text=How%20do%20cells%20distinguish%20between,thought%20to%20be%20devoid%20of%20activity (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== truncated O-GalNAc glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : truncated O-GalNAc glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-glycans initiated by GalNAc on serine or threonine residues that are incompletely extended, including structures such as Tn, STn, and T antigens, which are often associated with cancer. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=In%20addition%2C%20the%20core-fucosylated,residual%20disease%20after%20surgery (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== truncated O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : truncated O-glycan <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : incomplete O-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Truncated O-glycans are O-linked glycans that are not fully elongated or processed, often terminating in early core structures such as Tn antigen, T antigen, or their sialylated forms (STn, ST). These incompletely extended glycans frequently expose core structures and are commonly observed in carcinomas, where they can evoke immune responses. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Mucins%20might%20also%20interfere%20with,receptor%20%28CAR%29%20T-cell%20therapies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch47/#:~:text=Mucins%20might%20also%20interfere%20with,receptor%20%28CAR%29%20T-cell%20therapies (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== type-1 unit ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : type-1 unit <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galβ1-3GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | type-1 chain repeating unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A disaccharide motif consisting of galactose β1-3 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (Galβ1-3GlcNAc), known as the Type 1 chain. This structure can be repeated, serves as a scaffold for further glycan extension, and acts as a precursor for type 1 H and Lewis antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=Extensions%20by%20Type%201,labile%20under%20alkaline%20conditions (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== type-2 unit ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : type-2 unit <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galβ1-4GlcNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | type-2 chain repeating unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The type-2 unit is a disaccharide motif consisting of galactose β1-4 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (Galβ1-4GlcNAc), which can be repeated and serves as a scaffold for further glycan extension. It acts as a minimal acceptor for glycosyltransferases, is a ligand for galectins, and forms the backbone for the synthesis of blood group H, A, B, and Lewis antigens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== α-Gal epitope ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : α-Gal epitope <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :

=== β-D-xyloside ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : β-D-xyloside <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycoside in which a β-D-xylose residue is linked to an aglycone. β-D-xylosides can alter proteoglycan biosynthesis by serving as artificial primers for glycosaminoglycan chain initiation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== β-N-acetylgalactosaminide ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : β-N-acetylgalactosaminide <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Others (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycan structure featuring an N-acetylgalactosamine residue linked through a beta-glycosidic bond. These structures can serve as primers for oligosaccharide chains on mucins and are known to inhibit O-glycosylation of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

=== β6-linked N-acetylglucosamine ===

 '''Description''' :
 - A β(1-6)-linked N-acetylglucosamine residue that is transferred by core 2 β1-6GlcNAcT enzymes (CGNT1, 3, and 4) during the biosynthesis of core 2 O-GalNAc glycans. This residue often serves as the branching point for poly-LacNAc extensions in mucin glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== 3-O-sulfated GlcNS6S ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : 3-O-sulfated GlcNS6S <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glucosamine residue in heparin or heparan sulfate that is N-sulfated, 6-O-sulfated, and further modified by 3-O-sulfation. This specific modification, known as 3-O-sulfated GlcNS6S, is critical for antithrombin–heparin interaction and is also recognized by certain viruses, highlighting its functional significance in biological processes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Three%20structural%20elements%20that,diffraction%20to%202.6-%C3%85%20resolution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== 6-O-sulfated glucosamine ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : 6-O-sulfated glucosamine <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glucosamine residues within heparan sulfate chains that are sulfated at the 6-O position. These residues are subject to modification by endosulfatases (SULFs), which can alter their sulfation status and influence the biological properties of the glycan chain. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Apiogalacturonan is a class of plant cell wall pectic polysaccharides characterized by a galacturonic acid backbone with β-D-apiose (Apif) and apiobiose residues attached to the O-2 position of some backbone residues. === chondroitin ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : chondroitin <br>

=== chondroitin sulfate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : chondroitin sulfate <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000043 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : chondroitin polysulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | chondroitin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | chondroitin sulfate chain (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | chondroitin sulfates (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | chondroitin sulfuric acid (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | chondroitin sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Chondroitinsulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Any of a class of 10‚Äî60 kDa glycosaminoglycan sulfates, widely distributed in cartilage and other mammalian connective tissues.[CHEBI:37397] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Chondroitin sulfate is a sulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetylgalactosamine and glucuronic acid, with variable sulfation patterns. It commonly occurs as chains covalently attached to proteoglycans and can be found as oligosaccharide segments (typically 4–20 residues) derived from or mimicking portions of the full polysaccharide. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=A%20main%20technical%20challenge%20to,preference%20of%20GAG-binding%20proteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== dermatan sulfate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : dermatan sulfate <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000054 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : beta-Heparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Chondroitin sulfate B (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Dermatan L-iduronate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''Definition''' :
 - Any of a group of glycosaminoglycans with repeating units consisting of variously sulfated β1→4-linked L-iduronyl-(α1→3)-N-acetyl-D-galactosamine units.[CHEBI:18376] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Dermatan sulfate is a glycosaminoglycan (GAG) consisting of repeating disaccharide units of N-acetylgalactosamine and iduronic acid, which is formed by epimerization of glucuronic acid. The chain is modified by O-sulfation and is typically found as a sulfated polysaccharide component of proteoglycans. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== glycogen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glycogen <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycogen is a highly branched glucose polymer composed mainly of α(1-4)-linked glucose residues with α(1-6)-linked branch points. It serves as a major glucose storage polysaccharide in animals, allowing for rapid mobilization of glucose when needed. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== glycosaminoglycans ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : glycosaminoglycans <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GAGs (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are linear polysaccharides composed of repeating acidic disaccharide units, typically containing a hexosamine and a hexose or hexuronic acid. These chains are often sulfated and are covalently attached to core proteins to form proteoglycans. GAGs, which include heparan sulfate, chondroitin sulfate, dermatan sulfate, and others, are critical for protein interactions and the structure of the extracellular matrix in eukaryotes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Several%20viruses%20and%20bacteria%20also,chain%20%28e.g.%2C%20antithrombin%20binding%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Thus%2C%20to%20bind%20a%20GAG%20chain%2C%20the,under%20physiological%20conditions (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch38/#:~:text=Finally%2C%20one%20should%20keep%20in%20mind,inhibitor%20have%20GAG-binding%20sites (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch05/#:~:text=However%2C%20the%20import%20is,of%20misfolded%20glycoproteins (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== hemicellulose ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : hemicellulose <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Hemicellulosic polysaccharides (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Hemicellulose refers to a class of branched plant cell wall polysaccharides characterized by a backbone of 1-4-linked β-D-pyranosyl residues, such as glucose, mannose, or xylose. This group includes xylans, glucuronoxylans, galactomannans, and xyloglucans. Hemicelluloses are synthesized in the Golgi apparatus and secreted into the apoplast, where they contribute to the structure and function of plant cell walls. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=An%20independent%20clade%20of%20enzymes,chitin%2C%20and%20glycosaminoglycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== heparan sulfate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : heparan sulfate <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000106 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G17927IW <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G17927IW G17927IW] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G17927IW View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Heparan N-sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparan sulphate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparatan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparin monosulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparitin monosulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparitin sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | HS (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | N-Acetylheparan sulfate (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A linear polysaccharide in which the backbone consists of a variably sulfated repeating disaccharide unit.[CHEBI:28815] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Heparan sulfate is a glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of glucuronic acid (GlcA) and N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc), assembled as a copolymer and extensively modified by sulfation and epimerization. It is synthesized on a linkage tetrasaccharide attached to serine residues of core proteins, forming chains that are found on proteoglycans. Heparan sulfate oligosaccharides are short segments derived from or mimicking these chains. Heparan sulfate plays important roles in cell signaling and molecular interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - Heparan sulfate proteoglycans (HSPGs) play crucial roles regulating key developmental signaling pathways, such as the Wnt, Hedgehog, transforming growth factor-beta, and fibroblast growth factor pathways. (Source:  EP0086) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch17/#:~:text=Animal%20cells%20also%20degrade,arise%20from%20incomplete%20N-sulfation (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch03/#:~:text=Structural%20diversity%20of%20these,of%20a%20proper%20biological%20response (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== heparin ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : heparin <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000107 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : bemiparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | certoparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Cy 222 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | enoxaparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | fluxum (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Heparinic acid (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | parnaparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | reviparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | sandoparin (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A highly sulfated linear glycosaminoglycan comprising complex patterns of uronic acid-(1→4)-D-glucosamine repeating subunits. Used as an injectable anticoagulant, it has the highest negative charge density of any known biological molecule.[CHEBI:28304] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Heparin is a highly sulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units, typically containing N-sulfated glucosamine and iduronic acid, both variably sulfated. Structurally related to heparan sulfate, heparin chains are often attached to core proteins and are well known for their anticoagulant activity and high affinity for various proteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 '''Description''' :
 - Heteroxylan refers to a class of hemicellulosic polysaccharides found in the secondary cell walls of seed-producing plants. === hyaluronan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : hyaluronan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000183 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G29086KO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G29086KO G29086KO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G29086KO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GlcAβ1-3GlcNAcβ1-4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | HA (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Hyaluronan is a high-molecular-weight, linear, nonsulfated glycosaminoglycan composed of repeating disaccharide units of glucuronic acid (GlcA) β1-3 linked to N-acetylglucosamine (GlcNAc) β1-4. These long, polyanionic chains occupy large hydrodynamic volumes and play important roles in cell adhesion, signaling, and tissue structure. Short hyaluronan chains or oligosaccharides, generated by enzymatic cleavage, can interact with membrane proteins such as CD44 and TLR2/4, modulating signaling pathways and inducing biological effects including apoptosis in cancer cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== keratan sulfate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : keratan sulfate <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000114 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G82109MW <br>

 - Keratan sulfate (KS) is a glycosaminoglycan (GAG) composed of repeating disaccharide units of galactose and N-acetylglucosamine, which are variably sulfated. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Leishmania lipophosphoglycan repeat unit ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Leishmania lipophosphoglycan repeat unit <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Gal-Man-PO4- repeat unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Galβ1-4Manα1-PO4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide Repeating Unit (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A repeating disaccharide-phosphate unit composed of galactose (β1-4 linked to mannose) with a phosphate group, forming the backbone of the phosphoglycan polymer in Leishmania lipophosphoglycan (LPG) and related phosphoglycosylated protein glycans. This structural motif is characteristic of the phosphoglycan portions of LPG and proteophosphoglycan (PPG) in certain protozoa. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== lipoteichoic acid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : lipoteichoic acid <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : LTA (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Lipoteichoic acids (LTAs) are anionic glycopolymers present in the cell envelopes of Gram-positive bacteria. They typically consist of polyglycerol phosphate chains anchored to a glycolipid, and display diverse carbohydrate structures.  - Mannosylated lipoarabinomannan (ManLAM) is a form of lipoarabinomannan (LAM) found in mycobacteria, characterized by mannose residues capping its structure. === poly-N-acetyllactosamine ===

 '''Description''' :
 - Sulfated glycosaminoglycans (GAGs) are a class of linear polysaccharides that include heparan sulfate, chondroitin sulfate, dermatan sulfate, and keratan sulfate. These molecules are characterized by the presence of sulfate groups and are typically covalently attached to core proteins in proteoglycans. They play important roles in cell signaling, extracellular matrix structure, and protein interactions. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== xyloglucan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : xyloglucan <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G25326UC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G25326UC G25326UC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G25326UC View Structure] <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Xyloglucan is a major hemicellulosic polysaccharide found in plant cell walls, consisting of a β-1,4-glucan backbone substituted with α-D-xylose and sometimes other sugars. It plays a key role in cell wall structure and expansion by acting as a tether between cellulose microfibrils. Oligosaccharide fragments derived from the enzymatic or chemical breakdown of xyloglucan can trigger defense responses in plants and provide protection against pathogens. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== β-(1,3)-glucan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : β-(1,3)-glucan <br>

 '''Classification (beta)''' : Polysaccharide (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - β-(1,3)-glucan is a polysaccharide composed of glucose units linked by β-(1,3) glycosidic bonds. It can be linear or branched and is a major structural component of fungal and oomycete cell walls. β-(1,3)-glucan can also be released as oligosaccharides, which may function as pathogen-associated molecular patterns (PAMPs) in plants. === sialyl-Tn ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialyl-Tn <br>

 '''Definition''' :
 - N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminyl-(2→6)-N-acetyl-alpha-D-galactosamine is an N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminyl-(2→6)-N-acetyl-D-galactosamine in which the carbon bearing the anomeric hydroxy group has alpha configuration. It has a role as an epitope. It derives from a N-acetyl-alpha-D-galactosamine and a N-acetyl-alpha-neuraminic acid.[CHEBI:61818] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The Sialyl-Tn antigen is a glycan structure composed of N-acetylneuraminic acid (Neu5Ac) linked in an α2-6 configuration to N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) in α configuration. It is recognized as a tumor-associated carbohydrate antigen. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialyl-Tn antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialyl-Tn antigen <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : sTn (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialyl-Tn is a truncated O-glycan structure (Neu5Acα2-6GalNAcα-) formed by the sialylation of the Tn antigen. It is commonly overexpressed in various cancers and serves as a notable cancer-associated glycan epitope. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated biantennary N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated biantennary N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000154 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : sialyl biantennary (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A binantennary glycan with one or two sialic acid residues as non-reducing terminal residues on one or both antennae. [RN] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A type of N-linked glycan characterized by a core structure with two antennae (biantennary), each ending with sialic acid residues. These glycans are commonly found on human glycoproteins, including therapeutic proteins such as erythropoietin. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Human%20core%201%20O-glycan%20biosynthesis,S2%20Drosophila%20melanogaster%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Simbucus nigra agglutinin or an antibody specific to sialylated lactosamine with a preference for Neu5Acalpha2-6Gal rather than Neu5Acalpha2-3Gal reduced sperm binding to oviduct isthmic cells, as did occupying putative receptors on sperm with sialylated biantennary glycans.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/23115267 23115267]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated complex-type N-glycan <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000155 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : complex sialylated N-glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | complex-type sialylated glycan (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | sialo-complex type (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Complex N-linked glycan that have added GlcNAc residues at both the α-3 and α-6 mannose sites and do not contain mannose residues apart from the core structure but with sialic acid residues. (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Complex-type N-glycans that are modified by the addition of sialic acid residues at the terminal positions of their branches. These sialylated structures contribute to a negative charge, play roles in biological recognition, and are often used to enhance the circulatory half-life and alter the biodistribution of glycoproteins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - enhance the signaling activity of soluble intercellular adhesion molecule-1 in mouse astrocytes [PMID: 15201278] Appears in IgG-Fc. (Source: 20647032 ) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=The%20baculovirus-insect%20cell,add%20terminal%20sialic%20acids (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Combining%20knockout%20of%20St3gal4%20and,and%20improved%20biodistribution (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - By using 1H-NMR spectroscopy we were able to establish that our product contains a single N-linked biantennary , fully sialylated complex-type oligosaccharide , typical of human hepatomas.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1694115 1694115]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Publications''' : 6 references <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/11058758 11058758] <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15201278 15201278] <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/1694115 1694115] <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28342150 28342150] <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30471292 30471292] <br>

=== sialylated glycolipid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated glycolipid <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Glycolipids that contain sialic acid residues, which are recognized by myelin-associated glycoprotein and play a role in the inhibition of neuronal sprouting. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=The%20unusual%20polysialic%20acid%20chains,prevent%20neuronal%20regeneration (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated glycosphingolipid ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated glycosphingolipid <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Glycolipid (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialylated glycosphingolipids are glycosphingolipids that contain sialic acid residues. They are widely distributed in animal families, with the exception of arthropods, and are also referred to as gangliosides. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch26/#:~:text=Chitin%20is%20a%20major%20component%20of%20the,designated%20as%20the%20arthroseries (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated N-acetyllactosamine ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated N-acetyllactosamine <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialylated N-acetyllactosamine refers to N-acetyllactosamine disaccharides that have a terminal sialic acid residue. These structures are found in bacterial capsules and serve as targets for certain antibodies. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch20/#:~:text=Interestingly%2C%20most%20such%20examples,with%20a%20structural%20resemblance (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch46/#:~:text=This%20antibody%20is,sialylated%20blood%20group%20antigen%20N (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated N-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated N-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : N-Linked Type (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialylated N-glycans are N-linked glycans that possess terminal sialic acid residues. These structures are commonly found on glycoproteins, such as the IgG Fc domain, and play important roles in modulating antibody effector functions and other biological processes. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch41/#:~:text=Glycans%20are%20critical%20components%20of,underlying%20endothelial%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated O-GalNAc glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated O-GalNAc glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - O-linked glycans that are initiated by N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) and further modified by the addition of sialic acid residues, typically through the action of α2-3 sialyltransferases such as ST3GAL1. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch10/#:~:text=A%20family%20of%20%CE%B12-6,Gal%20residue%20of%20core%201%20O-glycans (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== sialylated O-glycan ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : sialylated O-glycan <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - Sialylated O-glycans are O-linked glycans that possess terminal sialic acid residues. These structures can be biosynthesized in engineered yeast expressing human sialyltransferases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch56/#:~:text=Examples%20of%20this%20include%20the%20hinge,O-glycan%20engineering%20in%20yeast (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== ceramide dihexosyl sulfate ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : ceramide dihexosyl sulfate <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000041 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G04416BG <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G04416BG G04416BG] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G04416BG View Structure] <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A sulfatide with two hexose moieties (glucose and galactose) has been isolated from human kidney (Martensson E, Acta Chem Scand 1963, 17, 1174). Equimolar amounts of ceramide, glucose, galactose, and sulfate are found in the molecule. The sulfate group is esterified, as in sulfatides, with carbon 3 of the galactose moiety. The linkage between galactose and glucose is identical to lactose (b-1-4). Its fatty acid composition is similar to that of sulfatides. [http://cyberlipid.gerli.com/description/complex-lipids/glycolipids/glycosphingolipids/sulfosphingolipids/] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - Although it has been reported that ceramide dihexosyl sulfate is present in human kidney, it is unlikely that it is present in equine kidney.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/818074 818074]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Publications''' : 1 references <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/818074 818074] <br>
 '''Database Cross-References''' :
 - [https://glycomotif.org/ GlycoMotif:GGM.000065] <br>

=== I antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : I antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000112 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G56045WU <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G56045WU G56045WU] <br>

 '''Exact Synonyms''' : branched poly-n-acetyllactosamine units (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - N-[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5S,6R)-4-[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-3-Acetamido-4-hydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5R,6R)-3,4,5-trihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxyoxan-2-yl]oxy-6-[[(2R,3R,4R,5S,6R)-3-acetamido-4-hydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)-5-[(2S,3R,4S,5R,6R)-3,4,5-trihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-2-yl]oxyoxan-2-yl]oxymethyl]-3,5-dihydroxyoxan-2-yl]oxy-2,4-dihydroxy-6-(hydroxymethyl)oxan-3-yl]acetamide (CHEBI:151390) is a amino sugar.[CHEBI:151390] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A branched poly-N-acetyllactosamine glycan determinant formed by β1-6 branching of repeating Galβ1-4GlcNAc units, distinguishing it from the linear i antigen. It is carried on N- and O-glycans and glycolipids, serves as part of the I blood group system, and its expression changes during development (i → I conversion) and in certain diseases. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

 - The I antigen is formed by the action of an enzyme (a glycosyltransferase), which adds branches onto the i antigen.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/15679458 15679458]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== O-antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : O-antigen <br>

=== P antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : P antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000088 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00061MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00061MO G00061MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00061MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : GLOB P antigen (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : Gb4 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : P1PK Blood Group System (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An amino tetrasaccharide consisting of 2-acetamido-2-deoxy-β-D-galactopyranosyl, α-D-galactopyranosyl, β-D-galactopyranosyl, and β-D-glucopyranose units joined together in sequence by (1→3), (1→4), and (1→4) glycosidic linkages, respectively.[CHEBI:145578] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - A glycosphingolipid antigen with the structure GalNAcβ1-3Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glcβ1-1Cer, belonging to the globoseries. It is the defining determinant of the P blood group system, carried on glycolipids of red blood cells and other tissues. Along with related glycans such as Gb3 (Pk antigen), P antigen can act as a receptor for microbial toxins and lectins, and has been implicated in cancer cell recognition and signaling. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Actinomycosis (Source: 18) <br>
 - Pyelonephritis (Source: 18) <br>
 - Acute cystitis (Source: 18) <br>
 - Shigellosis (Source: 18) <br>
 - Lazy leukocyte syndrome (Source: 18) <br>
 - Influenza (Source: 18) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=Interaction%20between%20prototype,Pk%20and%20P%20antigens%20%28Chapter%2014%29 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Here we report that PUGNAc treatment provokes globotetraosylceramide (Gb4Cer) accumulation in human umbilical vein endothelial cells (HUVEC).[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/28392398 28392398]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== P1 antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : P1 antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000141 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G12460DL | G55205ZV <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G12460DL G12460DL] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G12460DL View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : P1 determinant (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An amino pentasaccharide consisting of α-D-galactose, β-D-galactose N-acetyl-α-D-glucosamine, β-D-galactose, and β-D-glucose residues joined in sequence with ((1→4)-, (1→4)-, (1→3)- and (1→4)-linkages, respectively.[CHEBI:68484] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The P1 antigen is a specific glycan structure expressed on the urothelium, where it facilitates bacterial attachment and infection. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(a1-4)Gal(b1-4)GlcNAc(?1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - Shigellosis (Source: 160) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch14/#:~:text=This%20syndrome%20is%20distinct%20from%20CAD,following%20infection%20in%20utero (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - The P1 antigen was disovered in 1927 by Landsteiner and Levine, and Pk and NOR were described in 1951 and 1982, respectively.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24046920 24046920]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== Pk antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Pk antigen <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G06000DZ <br>

 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Related Synonyms''' : Gb3 (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : P1PK Blood Group System (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The Pk antigen is a type of glycosphingolipid containing a trisaccharide core structure with the sequence Galα1-4Galβ1-4Glc. It acts as a receptor for Shiga toxins. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>

=== Sda antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Sda antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000145 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G41052GC <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G41052GC G41052GC] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G41052GC View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : Sd(A) (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - A branched amino tetrasaccharide comprised of a trisaccharide chain of N-acetyl-α-neuraminic acid, β-D-galactose and N-acetyl-β-D-glucosamine residues linked sequentially (2→3) and (1→4), to the galactose residue of which is also linked (1→4) an N-acetyl-β-L-galactosamine residue. [CHEBI:71561] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The Sda antigen is a glycan motif defined by the terminal structure GalNAcβ1-4(Neu5Acα2-3)Galβ1-, which is recognized by specific monoclonal antibodies and found on certain glycoproteins and glycolipids. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

 - The expanding roles of the Sd(a)/Cad carbohydrate antigen and its cognate glycosyltransferase B4GALNT2.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/24112972 24112972]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>

=== T antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : T antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000163 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G00031MO <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G00031MO G00031MO] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G00031MO View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-GalNAc-Gal (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | TF antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E, GSD_GLYGEN_V0) | Thomsen-Fridenreich antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : O-Linked Core (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - An amino disaccharide composed of D-galactose and N-acetyl-α-D-galactosaminyl residues in β-(1→3) linkage.[CHEBI:61820] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The T antigen is a disaccharide glycan structure consisting of galactose (Gal) β1-3 linked to N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) O-linked to serine or threonine, representing the core 1 structure of mucin-type O-glycans. It is commonly found as a core 1 O-glycan on glycoproteins, recognized by certain lectins, and is associated with tumor cells. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>Gal(b1-3)GalNAc(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - accumulation Lipoprotein(a) or LDL within arterial walls in atherogenesis (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - cancer progression (increase interaction/communication of the cells) (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - T antigen expression in RMA lymphoma cells leads to a reduced growth of the tumor (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - correlation with elevated Golgi pH in cancer cell (breast, colorectal cancer cells) (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - T antigen on apical surfaces of corneal epithelial cells provide protection against rose bengal penetrance. (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - reduction of a flexibility of the glycosidic bond and thereby favour certain glycan conformation (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - T antigen is a target for NK cell-mediated lysis (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - inverse relationship between T antigen expression and VWF plasma levels (Source: EP0020) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - anaplastic meningioma (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - breast cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - colorectal cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - colon cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - esophageal cancer (Source: unknown) <br>
 - gastric cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - liver cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - ovarian cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - prostate cancer (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - bladder tumor (Source: EP0020) <br>
 - invasive pneumococcal infection (hemolytic uremic syndrome, hemolytic anemia) (Source: EP0020) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch51/#:~:text=ETD%20works%20reasonably%20well%20with,of%20prior%20glycopeptide%20enrichment (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/books/n/glyco4/ch31/#:~:text=R-type%20lectins%20are%20present%20in,of%20cultured%20mammalian%20tumor%20cells (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 - Tn, sTn, and T antigen neo- or over-expression occurs in many types of cancer including gastric, colon, breast, lung, esophageal, prostate, and endometrial cancer.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/27679419 27679419]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Publications''' : 50 references <br>
 - [https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/10555041 10555041] <br>

=== Tn antigen ===
 '''term (main_entry)''' : Tn antigen <br>
 '''GSD IDs''' : GSD000167 (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''GlyTouCan IDs''' : G57321FI <br>
 '''Image URL''' : [https://image.glycosmos.org/snfg/png/G57321FI G57321FI] <br>
 '''GlyTouCan''' : [https://glytoucan.org/Structures/Glycans/G57321FI View Structure] <br>
 '''Exact Synonyms''' : O-GalNAc (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) | Thomsen-Nouveau antigen (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Classification (beta)''' : Named Functional Motif (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''Definition''' :
 - Tn anitigen or alpha-galnac or N-Acetylgalactosamine is an N-acetyl-D-galactosamine having alpha-configuration at the anomeric centre. It has a role as an epitope. It derives from an alpha-D-galactosamine.[CHEBI:40356,HMDB0000212] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
 '''Description''' :
 - The Tn antigen is a single N-acetylgalactosamine (GalNAc) residue O-linked to serine or threonine on proteins. It represents the simplest O-GalNAc glycan structure and is the initial product of mucin-type O-glycosylation. (Source: EOG_VARKI_4E) <br>
 '''IUPAC Condensed''' :
 - <code>GalNAc(a1-</code> (Source: PUBDICTIONARIES-GLYCAN-IMAGE) <br>
 '''Biological Functions''' :
 - lymphatic metastasis (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - tube formation in Drosophila development (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - modulation of malignant phenotype (gastric carcinoma cell) (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - correlation with tumor stage, lymphatic invasion and lymph node matastasis (Source: EP0021) <br>
 '''Disease Associations''' :
 - breast cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - cervical cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - colon cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - colorectal cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - fibrosarcoma (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - gastric cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - liver cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - ovarian cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - pancreas cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - prostate cancer (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - Tn syndorome (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - Barrett'sesophagus (Source: EP0021) <br>
 - Schindler/Kanzaki desease (alpha-N acetylgalactosaminidase deficiency)[GlycoEpitope:EP0021]. (Source: unknown) <br>
 '''Evidence''' :

 - Furthermore, we confirmed that LS174T cells (Tn-positive) transfected with wild-type Cosmc, thus expressing no Tn antigen, had down-regulation of H-Ras expression and subsequent inhibition of EMT process.[PMID:[[https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30637914 30637914]]] (Source: GSD_GLYGEN_V0) <br>
